<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Let a finite-state streaming learner estimate a uniformly random unit vector from independent exact Gaussian linear measurements. We prove that $`o(d^2)`$ bits of persistent memory and angular success probability at least 2/3 require at least $`2^{-16}d\log_2(1/\epsilon)`$ samples for all sufficiently large <i>d</i>, uniformly for $`0\lt \epsilon\le1/10`$. The proof conditions each batch on its observed projection and controls the resulting random residual subsphere.

- **claim-precision-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-main)): Let $M:\mathbb N\to\mathbb Z_{\ge0}$ satisfy $M(d)=o(d^2)$. There is a dimension threshold depending only on this memory sequence such that, for every larger $d$, every $0<\epsilon\le1/10$, and every learner in Definition <a href="#s:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="s:model">1</a>, the following implication holds. If $S$ is uniform on $S^{d-1}$, independently of the Gaussian rows and learner randomness, and $$\mathbb P\{\arccos\langle \widehat s,S\rangle\le\epsilon\}\ge\frac23,$$ then $$\label{s:main-bound} T\ge 2^{-16}d\log_2(1/\epsilon).$$
  - Explanation: With subquadratic persistent memory, any finite-state streaming learner that reaches the stated angular accuracy with probability at least 2/3 needs a number of samples proportional to d times the base-two logarithm of 1/epsilon, with an explicit coefficient, once the dimension is large.
- **claim-worst-signal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-worst-signal)): Under the memory assumption of Theorem <a href="#s:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="s:main">2</a>, suppose a learner satisfies $$\mathbb P_s\{\arccos\langle \widehat s,s\rangle\le\epsilon\}\ge\frac23 \qquad\text{for every }s\in S^{d-1},$$ where $\mathbb P_s$ averages its observations and randomness at the fixed signal $s$. Then <a href="#s:main-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="s:main-bound">[s:main-bound]</a> holds at the same dimension threshold.
  - Explanation: A guarantee holding at every fixed signal can be integrated over a uniform signal, so the same sample lower bound applies with the same dimension threshold.
- **claim-borel-versions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-borel)): Fix $d\ge2$, a finite horizon, and finite state sets. For almost every realization of the shared rule seed in Definition <a href="#s:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="s:model">1</a>, there are Borel transition and stopping kernels with the same state sets and terminal output laws whose conditional joint law of the uniform signal, state path, stopping index, and output is unchanged.
  - Explanation: Completed-measurable transition and stopping rules can be replaced by Borel kernels without changing the joint law of signal, state path, stopping index and output under the uniform prior.
- **claim-uniform-coordinates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-coordinates)): Let $E=E_1\oplus E_2$ be an orthogonal decomposition with $\dim E=D$, $\dim E_1=l$, and $1\le l<D$. If $U\sim\sigma_E$, then $$\left\lVert P_{E_1}U\right\rVert^2\sim\operatorname{Beta}(l/2,(D-l)/2),$$ and the two component directions are independent uniform directions, independent of this fraction. In coordinates on $E_1$, the projection $P_{E_1}U$ has density $$\label{s:coordinate-density} c(D,l)(1-\left\lVert t\right\rVert^2)^{(D-l-2)/2}\mathbf 1_{\{\left\lVert t\right\rVert<1\}}, \qquad c(D,l)=\frac{\Gamma(D/2)} {\pi^{l/2}\Gamma((D-l)/2)}.$$ Conditional on that projection, the complementary direction is uniform on $S(E_2)$. When $l=0$ or $l=D$, the squared projection is respectively the point mass at $0$ or at $1$.
  - Explanation: The squared length of a projection of a uniform direction onto a subspace is beta distributed, the two component directions are independent and uniform, and the projection has an explicit density.
- **claim-beta-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-beta-identities)): For $A,B>0$ and $Z\sim\operatorname{Beta}(A,B)$, a real $q\ge0$ satisfies $$\label{s:beta-real} \mathbb EZ^{-q} =\frac{\mathrm B(A-q,B)}{\mathrm B(A,B)} =\frac{\Gamma(A-q)\Gamma(A+B)} {\Gamma(A)\Gamma(A+B-q)} \quad\text{if }q<A.$$ The moment diverges when $q\ge A$. If $h$ is a nonnegative integer with $h<A$, then $$\label{s:beta-integer} \mathbb EZ^{-h}=\prod_{t=1}^h\frac{A+B-t}{A-t},$$ where the product for $h=0$ is one. Moreover, for $C>0$, independent $$Z\sim\operatorname{Beta}(A,B),\qquad X\sim\operatorname{Beta}(A+B,C)$$ satisfy $$\label{s:beta-product} ZX\sim\operatorname{Beta}(A,B+C).$$
  - Explanation: Gives exact inverse moments of a beta variable, an integer-exponent product form, and closure of beta laws under multiplication of independent factors.
- **claim-residual-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-residual)): Let $s=z+r\omega$ with $\omega\sim\sigma_H$, independently of a matrix $X$ with $b$ independent standard Gaussian rows. Assume $z\perp H$, $r>0$, and $\left\lVert z\right\rVert^2+r^2=1$. Put $D=\dim H$, and let $1\le b<D$. Let $R_X$ be the row span of $X$ restricted to $H$, and set $K=H\cap R_X^\perp$. Conditional on $X$, the joint experiment has the representation $$\label{s:residual-representation} s=z+r\sqrt{1-t}\,e+r\sqrt t\,v, \quad t\sim\operatorname{Beta}((D-b)/2,b/2),\quad e\sim\sigma_{R_X},\quad v\sim\sigma_K,$$ where the three draws are independent. The labels are $$Xs=X\bigl(z+r\sqrt{1-t}\,e\bigr).$$ Marginally, $t$ is independent of $(e,K)$, $e$ is uniform on $S(H)$, and, conditional on $e$, $K$ is uniform among the $(D-b)$-dimensional subspaces of $H\cap e^\perp$.
  - Explanation: Conditioning an affine subsphere target on a Gaussian block leaves an observed row-space component plus a uniform direction in a random residual subspace, with a beta distributed squared radius fraction.
- **claim-state-maximum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-state-maximum)): In the setting of Lemma <a href="#s:residual" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="s:residual">7</a>, let $\mathcal V$ be nonempty and finite, let $g_v:S^{d-1}\to[0,1]$ be Borel, and let Borel weights $\kappa_v(X,y)\ge0$ satisfy $\sum_v\kappa_v\le1$. Put $$z'=z+r\sqrt{1-t}\,e,\qquad \rho=r\sqrt t,\qquad F_v(K)=\int g_v(z'+\rho u)\,d\sigma_K(u).$$ Then $$\label{s:state-max-bound} \mathbb E_{X,s}\sum_v\kappa_v(X,Xs)g_v(s) \le \mathbb E_{t,e}\mathbb E_{K\mid e}\max_v F_v(K).$$ The expectation on the left uses the independent uniform subsphere target and Gaussian block.
  - Explanation: After a block, the averaged continuation success is bounded by the expected maximum over continuation states of the subsphere averages, so data-dependent state choice is charged before the matrix is averaged away.
- **claim-spherical-caps** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-caps)): Let $e$ be a unit vector in a $D$-dimensional Euclidean space. For $D\ge3$ and every $\delta>0$, $$\label{s:sharp-cap} \sigma_{\mathbb R^D}\{u:\left\lVert u-e\right\rVert\le\delta\} \le \frac{c_D}{2}\delta^{D-1} \le \frac{\sqrt D}{2}\delta^{D-1}.$$ For $D\ge2$ and $0\le\theta\le\pi$, $$\label{s:angular-cap} \sigma_{\mathbb R^D}\{u:\angle(u,e)\le\theta\} \le(2\theta)^{D-1}.$$ Consequently, on an affine subsphere $z+R S(H)$ as in <a href="#s:affine-sphere" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="s:affine-sphere">[s:affine-sphere]</a>, with radius $R$ and linear dimension $D\ge2$, the angular success probability of any fixed unit output is at most $$\label{s:affine-terminal} \min\{1,(8\epsilon/R)^{D-1}\}.$$ The last assertion also holds after averaging any target-independent output law.
  - Explanation: Chordal and angular cap masses are bounded explicitly, which bounds the angular success probability of any fixed output on an affine subsphere.
- **claim-conditional-direction-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-directions)): Let $E$ have dimension $N$, let $L$ be a uniform $K$-dimensional subspace, and assume integers $1\le h<K\le N$. Conditional on $L$, draw $Y_1,\ldots,Y_h$ independently with law $\sigma_L$. Given $Y_1,\ldots,Y_j$, their span $V$ has dimension $j$ almost surely, and $L$ is uniform among the $K$-subspaces containing $V$. For $1\le j<h$, the conditional density of $Y_{j+1}$ relative to $\sigma_E$ is $$\label{s:direction-density} \frac{c(K,j)}{c(N,j)} \operatorname{dist}(y,V)^{-(N-K)} \le \operatorname{dist}(y,V)^{-(N-K)}.$$ For $j=0$, the first marginal is $\sigma_E$. If $K=N$, the density in <a href="#s:direction-density" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="s:direction-density">[s:direction-density]</a> is identically one.
  - Explanation: Directions drawn independently in one uniform random subspace have, given the earlier ones, a conditional density relative to the uniform law that blows up as an inverse power of the distance to their span.
- **claim-terminal-count** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-terminal-count)): For $d\ge3$, a learner of horizon $T$ satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb P(\mathrm{success}) &\le (T+1)W(c_d/2)\epsilon^{d-1},\label{s:output-count-sharp}\\ \mathbb P(\mathrm{success}) &\le (T+1)W(2\epsilon)^{d-1}.\label{s:output-count-angular} \end{aligned}$$ The learner can be padded to any prescribed later sample layer, with the same success law and width at most $$\label{s:padded-width} W_*=(T+2)W.$$
  - Explanation: Success probability is bounded by the number of terminal index-state pairs times a cap mass, and a learner can be padded to a later layer with a controlled width.
- **claim-short-run-budget** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-short-run)): Fix constants $c>0$ and $\alpha>0$, and put $L=\log_2(1/\epsilon)$. Suppose $M(d)=o(d^2)$. On any sequence of dimensions where uniform-prior success is at least $\alpha$ and $T\le cdL$, one has $$\label{s:short-run-conclusions} L=o(d),\qquad T=o(d^2),\qquad \log_2 W_*=o(d^2).$$ Consequently, for any block length $k\ge c_0d$ with fixed $c_0>0$, $\lceil T/k\rceil=o(d)$ on that sequence.
  - Explanation: On dimensions where a short run still succeeds, the precision parameter is sublinear in d, the horizon is subquadratic and the padded log-width is subquadratic, so few blocks are needed.
- **claim-full-data-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-full-data)): Let $0\le T\le d-3$. Give an estimator all $T$ Gaussian rows and labels for a uniform signal, and allow any unit output depending on these data and independent randomness. Put $D=d-T$. For every $0<r_0<1$, its angular success probability is at most $$\label{s:full-data-bound} \frac{T}{d(1-r_0^2)} +\min\left\{1,\ \frac{c_D}{2}(2\epsilon/r_0)^{D-1},\ (8\epsilon/r_0)^{D-1}\right\}.$$
  - Explanation: An estimator that sees all rows and labels still has bounded success when fewer rows than dimensions are available, because a residual direction remains uniform.
- **claim-two-component-domination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-mixture)): Let $E$ have dimension $D'$, let $V\subset E$ have dimension $1\le j<n<D'$, and let $N$ be uniform among the $n$-dimensional subspaces containing $V$. Conditional on $N$, let $v$ be uniform on $S(N)$. Set $$\alpha=\frac{n-j}{2},\qquad \alpha_*=\frac{D'-j}{2},\qquad \beta_0=\frac j2,\qquad \beta_1=\frac{D'-n+j-1}{2}.$$ Let $\nu_0=\sigma_E$. Let $\nu_1$ be the law of $$\label{s:mixture-construction} \sqrt{1-Z}\,e_0+\sqrt Z\,u_0,\qquad Z\sim\operatorname{Beta}(\alpha,\alpha_*-\alpha),\quad e_0\sim\sigma_V,\quad u_0\mid e_0\sim\sigma_{E\cap e_0^\perp},$$ where $Z$ is independent of the directions. Then, as positive measures on $S(E)$, $$\label{s:mixture-domination} \operatorname{Law}(v)\le2^{D'}(\nu_0+\nu_1).$$ For $j=0$, the first direction averaged over a uniform $N$ is uniform on $S(E)$. If $n=D'$, then $N=E$ and each new independent direction is uniform even after earlier directions are conditioned on. These cases require no mixture.
  - Explanation: The law of the next direction in a random subspace containing the earlier span is dominated by an exponential factor times a mixture of the uniform law and a latitude law.
- **claim-weighted-block** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-weighted-theorem)): There are absolute constants $C\ge1$ and $d_*$ such that the following holds for every integer $d\ge d_*$. Put $$k=m=\lfloor d/16\rfloor,\qquad a=d/16.$$ Let $W\ge1$, let $\mathcal V$ be a nonempty finite set with $\|\mathcal V\|\le W$, and let $\kappa_v:\mathbb R^{k\times d}\times\mathbb R^k\to[0,1]$ be Borel functions with $\sum_{v\in\mathcal V}\kappa_v\le1$. For Borel $g_v:S^{d-1}\to[0,1]$, set $$g_{\rm in}(s)= \mathbb E_X\sum_{v\in\mathcal V}\kappa_v(X,Xs)g_v(s),$$ where $X$ has $k$ independent $N(0,I_d)$ rows. For every integer $n$ with $d/2\le n\le d$, $$\label{s:weighted-block} B_n(g_{\rm in}) \le W^{1/m}C^d\max_{v\in\mathcal V}B_{n-2}(g_v).$$ If $\epsilon>0$, $\pi$ is any probability measure on $S^{d-1}$ and $$g_\pi(s)=\int\mathbf 1_{\{\arccos\langle u,s\rangle\le\epsilon\}}\,\pi(du),$$ then, in the same range of $n$, $$\label{s:weighted-terminal} B_n(g_\pi)\le C^d\epsilon^a.$$
  - Explanation: A block selecting among at most W continuations raises the radius-weighted subsphere norm by at most a width factor times an exponential constant, while lowering the test dimension by two; a terminal bound follows from caps.
- **claim-cap-moment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-cap-moment)): Let $E$ have dimension $N\le d$, and let $L$ be a uniform $K$-space in $E$, with integers $1\le h<K\le N$. Put $b=N-K$. Let $f:S(E)\to[0,1]$ be Borel. Suppose that for some finite $\Lambda\ge0$, constants $A_0,A_1\ge1$, and $0\le a<N-1$, $$\begin{aligned} \int f\,d\sigma_E&\le A_0^d\Lambda,\label{s:cap-total}\\ \int_{\left\lVert y-y_0\right\rVert\le\delta}f(y)\,d\sigma_E(y) &\le A_1^d\Lambda\delta^{N-1-a} \quad(y_0\in S(E),\ \delta>0).\label{s:cap-hypothesis} \end{aligned}$$ If $$\label{s:moment-margin} N-1-a-(h-1)-b\ge1,$$ then $$\label{s:moment-bound} \mathbb E_L\left(\int f\,d\sigma_L\right)^h \le\left([4\max\{A_0,9A_1\}]^d\Lambda\right)^h.$$ For a nonempty family of at most $W$ functions satisfying the same conditions with numbers $\Lambda_v$, $$\label{s:moment-maximum} \mathbb E_L\max_v\int f_v\,d\sigma_L \le W^{1/h}[4\max\{A_0,9A_1\}]^d\max_v\Lambda_v.$$ In particular, $A_0=1,A_1=4$ gives the constant $144^d$.
  - Explanation: Cap hypotheses with enough dimension slack make the singular conditional density integrable, giving moment and maximum bounds for uniform subspace averages.
- **claim-weighted-application** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-weighted-application)): There is an absolute $c>0$ such that, for every $M(d)=o(d^2)$, all sufficiently large $d$, and all $0<\epsilon\le1/10$, a learner with uniform-prior angular success at least $1/2$ satisfies $$T\ge c\,d\log_2(1/\epsilon).$$ The eventual threshold may depend on the memory sequence.
  - Explanation: Iterating the radius-weighted block estimate gives the same precision rate at success one half, with an unspecified absolute coefficient.
- **claim-affine-suffix-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-affine-proposition)): For all sufficiently large $d$, consider a Borel learner padded to $k$ blocks of $m$ exact Gaussian samples, of width at most $W$, and with target-independent unit output laws at the last layer. The randomized experiment, shared seed, initialization, and fresh randomness satisfy the joint measurability, independence, and information restrictions of Definition <a href="#s:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="s:model">1</a>. Here $0<\epsilon\le1/10$, and success means angular error at most $\epsilon$. Assume $$\label{s:affine-budget} n_0+2k\le d,\qquad W^{1/p}\le2^d.$$ For each fixed shared seed whose rules satisfy these assumptions, and for a state $v$ at layer $(k-\ell)m$, let $g_v(s)$ be the success probability of the suffix started in that state. For $0\le\ell\le k$, every affine subsphere <a href="#s:affine-sphere" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="s:affine-sphere">[s:affine-sphere]</a> with $\dim H=n\ge n_0+2\ell$ satisfies $$\label{s:affine-invariant-bound} \int g_v(z+r\xi)\,d\sigma_H(\xi) \le (R_\ell/r)^a,\qquad R_\ell=16C_*^\ell\epsilon.$$ The assertion is uniform in the fixed seed and includes states unreachable from the initial layer.
  - Explanation: An explicit bound on suffix success holds simultaneously on all admissible affine subspheres, degrading by a fixed factor per block.
- **claim-fixed-length-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-fixed-proposition)): There are absolute constants $C\ge2$ and $d_*$ such that the following holds for every integer $d\ge d_*$. Set $$k=p=\lfloor d/32\rfloor,\qquad \gamma=d/32.$$ Let $j\ge0$ and $m$ be integers with $d/2+2j\le m\le d$. Consider a fixed-length learner with $jk$ independent exact Gaussian samples in dimension $m$, Borel rules, and at most $2^{d^2}$ states at each layer. Its output may be any vector in $\mathbb R^m$. Its rules may use arbitrary fixed parameters independent of the target. Its randomized experiment, initialization, any shared rule seed, and fresh randomness obey the joint measurability, independence, and information restrictions in Definition <a href="#s:model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="s:model">1</a>, with $m$ in place of $d$. If the target $s$ is uniform on $S^{m-1}$, then for every $\eta>0$, $$\label{s:fixed-bound} \mathbb P\{\left\lVert \mathrm{output}-s\right\rVert\le\eta\} \le(C^{j+1}\eta)^\gamma.$$
  - Explanation: For fixed-length learners in varying dimensions with arbitrary Euclidean output, the success probability at Euclidean tolerance eta is bounded by a power of a constant times eta, proved via latitude simulation.

        "anchor": "stmt-s-state-maximum",
        "exact_quote": "In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#s:residual\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"s:residual\">7</a>, let $\\mathcal V$ be nonempty and finite, let $g_v:S^{d-1}\\to[0,1]$ be Borel, and let Borel weights $\\kappa_v(X,y)\\ge0$ satisfy $\\sum_v\\kappa_v\\le1$. Put $$z'=z+r\\sqrt{1-t}\\,e,\\qquad \\rho=r\\sqrt t,\\qquad\n F_v(K)=\\int g_v(z'+\\rho u)\\,d\\sigma_K(u).$$ Then $$\\label{s:state-max-bound}\n \\mathbb E_{X,s}\\sum_v\\kappa_v(X,Xs)g_v(s)\n \\le \\mathbb E_{t,e}\\mathbb E_{K\\mid e}\\max_v F_v(K).$$ The expectation on the left uses the independent uniform subsphere target and Gaussian block.",

      },

      "exact_statement": "In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#s:residual\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"s:residual\">7</a>, let $\\mathcal V$ be nonempty and finite, let $g_v:S^{d-1}\\to[0,1]$ be Borel, and let Borel weights $\\kappa_v(X,y)\\ge0$ satisfy $\\sum_v\\kappa_v\\le1$.         "anchor": "stmt-s-caps",
        "exact_quote": "Let $e$ be a unit vector in a $D$-dimensional Euclidean space. For $D\\ge3$ and every $\\delta>0$, $$\\label{s:sharp-cap}\n \\sigma_{\\mathbb R^D}\\{u:\\left\\lVert u-e\\right\\rVert\\le\\delta\\}\n \\le \\frac{c_D}{2}\\delta^{D-1}\n \\le \\frac{\\sqrt D}{2}\\delta^{D-1}.$$ For $D\\ge2$ and $0\\le\\theta\\le\\pi$, $$\\label{s:angular-cap}\n \\sigma_{\\mathbb R^D}\\{u:\\angle(u,e)\\le\\theta\\}\n \\le(2\\theta)^{D-1}.$$ Consequently, on an affine subsphere $z+R S(H)$ as in <a href=\"#s:affine-sphere\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"s:affine-sphere\">[s:affine-sphere]</a>, with radius $R$ and linear dimension $D\\ge2$, the angular success probability of any fixed unit output is at most $$\\label{s:affine-terminal}\n \\min\\{1,(8\\epsilon/R)^{D-1}\\}.$$ The last assertion also holds after averaging any target-independent output law.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.6"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-s-directions",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E$ have dimension $N$, let $L$ be a uniform $K$-dimensional subspace, and assume integers $1\\le h<K\\le N$. Conditional on $L$, draw $Y_1,\\ldots,Y_h$ independently with law $\\sigma_L$. Given $Y_1,\\ldots,Y_j$, their span $V$ has dimension $j$ almost surely, and $L$ is uniform among the $K$-subspaces containing $V$. For $1\\le j<h$, the conditional density of $Y_{j+1}$ relative to $\\sigma_E$ is $$\\label{s:direction-density}\n \\frac{c(K,j)}{c(N,j)}\n \\operatorname{dist}(y,V)^{-(N-K)}\n \\le \\operatorname{dist}(y,V)^{-(N-K)}.$$ For $j=0$, the first marginal is $\\sigma_E$. If $K=N$, the density in <a href=\"#s:direction-density\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"s:direction-density\">[s:direction-density]</a> is identically one.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-s-directions",
          "exact_quote": "Let $E$ have dimension $N$, let $L$ be a uniform $K$-dimensional subspace, and assume integers $1\\le h<K\\le N$. Given $Y_1,\\ldots,Y_j$, their span $V$ has dimension $j$ almost surely, and $L$ is uniform among the $K$-subspaces containing $V$. For $1\\le j<h$, the conditional density of $Y_{j+1}$ relative to $\\sigma_E$ is $$\\label{s:direction-density}\n \\frac{c(K,j)}{c(N,j)}\n \\operatorname{dist}(y,V)^{-(N-K)}\n \\le \\operatorname{dist}(y,V)^{-(N-K)}.$$ For $j=0$, the first marginal is $\\sigma_E$. If $K=N$, the density in <a href=\"#s:direction-density\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"s:direction-density\">[s:direction-density]</a> is identically one.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.6"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-s-terminal-count",
        "exact_quote": "For $d\\ge3$, a learner of horizon $T$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb P(\\mathrm{success})\n &\\le (T+1)W(c_d/2)\\epsilon^{d-1},\\label{s:output-count-sharp}\\\\\n \\mathbb P(\\mathrm{success})\n &\\le (T+1)W(2\\epsilon)^{d-1}.\\label{s:output-count-angular}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The learner can be padded to any prescribed later sample layer, with the same success law and width at most $$\\label{s:padded-width}\n W_*=(T+2)W.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-s-terminal-count",
          "exact_quote": "For $d\\ge3$, a learner of horizon $T$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb P(\\mathrm{success})\n &\\le (T+1)W(c_d/2)\\epsilon^{d-1},\\label{s:output-count-sharp}\\\\\n \\mathbb P(\\mathrm{success})\n &\\le (T+1)W(2\\epsilon)^{d-1}.\\label{s:output-count-angular}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The learner can be padded to any prescribed later sample layer, with the same success law and width at most $$\\label{s:padded-width}\n W_*=(T+2)W.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.6"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $d\\ge3$, a learner of horizon $T$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb P(\\mathrm{success})\n &\\le (T+1)W(c_d/2)\\epsilon^{d-1},\\label{s:output-count-sharp}\\\\\n \\mathbb P(\\mathrm{success})\n &\\le (T+1)W(2\\epsilon)^{d-1}.\\label{s:output-count-angular}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The learner can be padded to any prescribed later sample layer, with the same success law and width at most $$\\label{s:padded-width}\n W_*=(T+2)W.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-s-short-run",
        "exact_quote": "Fix constants $c>0$ and $\\alpha>0$, and put $L=\\log_2(1/\\epsilon)$. Suppose $M(d)=o(d^2)$. On any sequence of dimensions where uniform-prior success is at least $\\alpha$ and $T\\le cdL$, one has $$\\label{s:short-run-conclusions}\n L=o(d),\\qquad T=o(d^2),\\qquad \\log_2 W_*=o(d^2).$$ Consequently, for any block length $k\\ge c_0d$ with fixed $c_0>0$, $\\lceil T/k\\rceil=o(d)$ on that sequence.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-s-short-run",

          "source_id": "PM-140.6"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-s-full-data",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le T\\le d-3$. Give an estimator all $T$ Gaussian rows and labels for a uniform signal, and allow any unit output depending on these data and independent randomness.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-s-mixture",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E$ have dimension $D'$, let $V\\subset E$ have dimension $1\\le j<n<D'$, and let $N$ be uniform among the $n$-dimensional subspaces containing $V$. Conditional on $N$, let $v$ be uniform on $S(N)$. Set $$\\alpha=\\frac{n-j}{2},\\qquad\n \\alpha_*=\\frac{D'-j}{2},\\qquad\n \\beta_0=\\frac j2,\\qquad\n \\beta_1=\\frac{D'-n+j-1}{2}.$$ Let $\\nu_0=\\sigma_E$. Let $\\nu_1$ be the law of $$\\label{s:mixture-construction}\n \\sqrt{1-Z}\\,e_0+\\sqrt Z\\,u_0,\\qquad\n Z\\sim\\operatorname{Beta}(\\alpha,\\alpha_*-\\alpha),\\quad\n e_0\\sim\\sigma_V,\\quad\n u_0\\mid e_0\\sim\\sigma_{E\\cap e_0^\\perp},$$ where $Z$ is independent of the directions. Then, as positive measures on $S(E)$, $$\\label{s:mixture-domination}\n \\operatorname{Law}(v)\\le2^{D'}(\\nu_0+\\nu_1).$$ For $j=0$, the first direction averaged over a uniform $N$ is uniform on $S(E)$. If $n=D'$, then $N=E$ and each new independent direction is uniform even after earlier directions are conditioned on. These cases require no mixture.",
        "source_id": "PM-140.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-s-mixture",
          "exact_quote": "Let $E$ have dimension $D'$, let $V\\subset E$ have dimension $1\\le j<n<D'$, and let $N$ be uniform among the $n$-dimensional subspaces containing $V$. Set $$\\alpha=\\frac{n-j}{2},\\qquad\n \\alpha_*=\\frac{D'-j}{2},\\qquad\n \\beta_0=\\frac j2,\\qquad\n \\beta_1=\\frac{D'-n+j-1}{2}.$$ Let $\\nu_0=\\sigma_E$. Let $\\nu_1$ be the law of $$\\label{s:mixture-construction}\n \\sqrt{1-Z}\\,e_0+\\sqrt Z\\,u_0,\\qquad\n Z\\sim\\operatorname{Beta}(\\alpha,\\alpha_*-\\alpha),\\quad\n e_0\\sim\\sigma_V,\\quad\n u_0\\mid e_0\\sim\\sigma_{E\\cap e_0^\\perp},$$ where $Z$ is independent of the directions. Then, as positive measures on $S(E)$, $$\\label{s:mixture-domination}\n \\operatorname{Law}(v)\\le2^{D'}(\\nu_0+\\nu_1).$$ For $j=0$, the first direction averaged over a uniform $N$ is uniform on $S(E)$. If $n=D'$, then $N=E$ and each new independent direction is uniform even after earlier directions are conditioned on. These cases require no mixture.",
          "source_id": "PM-140.6"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $E$ have dimension $D'$, let $V\\subset E$ have dimension $1\\le j<n<D'$, and let $N$ be uniform among the $n$-dimensional subspaces containing $V$. Set $$\\alpha=\\frac{n-j}{2},\\qquad\n \\alpha_*=\\frac{D'-j}{2},\\qquad\n \\beta_0=\\frac j2,\\qquad\n \\beta_1=\\frac{D'-n+j-1}{2}.$$ Let $\\nu_0=\\sigma_E$. Let $\\nu_1$ be the law of $$\\label{s:mixture-construction}\n \\sqrt{1-Z}\\,e_0+\\sqrt Z\\,u_0,\\qquad\n Z\\sim\\operatorname{Beta}(\\alpha,\\alpha_*-\\alpha),\\quad\n e_0\\sim\\sigma_V,\\quad\n u_0\\mid e_0\\sim\\sigma_{E\\cap e_0^\\perp},$$ where $Z$ is independent of the directions. Then, as positive measures on $S(E)$, $$\\label{s:mixture-domination}\n \\operatorname{Law}(v)\\le2^{D'}(\\nu_0+\\nu_1).$$ For $j=0$, the first direction averaged over a uniform $N$ is uniform on $S(E)$. If $n=D'$, then $N=E$ and each new independent direction is uniform even after earlier directions are conditioned on. These cases require no mixture.",
      "explanation": "The law of the next direction in a random subspace containing the earlier span is dominated by an exponential factor times a mixture of the uniform law and a latitude law.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-s-weighted-theorem",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C\\ge1$ and $d_*$ such that the following holds for every integer $d\\ge d_*$. Put $$k=m=\\lfloor d/16\\rfloor,\\qquad a=d/16.$$ Let $W\\ge1$, let $\\mathcal V$ be a nonempty finite set with $|\\mathcal V|\\le W$, and let $\\kappa_v:\\mathbb R^{k\\times d}\\times\\mathbb R^k\\to[0,1]$ be Borel functions with $\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V}\\kappa_v\\le1$. For Borel $g_v:S^{d-1}\\to[0,1]$, set $$g_{\\rm in}(s)=\n \\mathbb E_X\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V}\\kappa_v(X,Xs)g_v(s),$$ where $X$ has $k$ independent $N(0,I_d)$ rows. For every integer $n$ with $d/2\\le n\\le d$, $$\\label{s:weighted-block}\n B_n(g_{\\rm in})\n \\le W^{1/m}C^d\\max_{v\\in\\mathcal V}B_{n-2}(g_v).$$ If $\\epsilon>0$, $\\pi$ is any probability measure on $S^{d-1}$ and $$g_\\pi(s)=\\int\\mathbf 1_{\\{\\arccos\\langle u,s\\rangle\\le\\epsilon\\}}\\,\\pi(du),$$ then, in the same range of $n$, $$\\label{s:weighted-terminal}\n B_n(g_\\pi)\\le C^d\\epsilon^a.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-140.6"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-s-weighted-theorem",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C\\ge1$ and $d_*$ such that the following holds for every integer $d\\ge d_*$. Put $$k=m=\\lfloor d/16\\rfloor,\\qquad a=d/16.$$ Let $W\\ge1$, let $\\mathcal V$ be a nonempty finite set with $|\\mathcal V|\\le W$, and let $\\kappa_v:\\mathbb R^{k\\times d}\\times\\mathbb R^k\\to[0,1]$ be Borel functions with $\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V}\\kappa_v\\le1$. For Borel $g_v:S^{d-1}\\to[0,1]$, set $$g_{\\rm in}(s)=\n \\mathbb E_X\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V}\\kappa_v(X,Xs)g_v(s),$$ where $X$ has $k$ independent $N(0,I_d)$ rows. For every integer $n$ with $d/2\\le n\\le d$, $$\\label{s:weighted-block}\n B_n(g_{\\rm in})\n \\le W^{1/m}C^d\\max_{v\\in\\mathcal V}B_{n-2}(g_v).$$ If $\\epsilon>0$, $\\pi$ is any probability measure on $S^{d-1}$ and $$g_\\pi(s)=\\int\\mathbf 1_{\\{\\arccos\\langle u,s\\rangle\\le\\epsilon\\}}\\,\\pi(du),$$ then, in the same range of $n$, $$\\label{s:weighted-terminal}\n B_n(g_\\pi)\\le C^d\\epsilon^a.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-140.6"
        }

Suppose that for some finite $\\Lambda\\ge0$, constants $A_0,A_1\\ge1$, and $0\\le a<N-1$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int f\\,d\\sigma_E&\\le A_0^d\\Lambda,\\label{s:cap-total}\\\\\n \\int_{\\left\\lVert y-y_0\\right\\rVert\\le\\delta}f(y)\\,d\\sigma_E(y)\n &\\le A_1^d\\Lambda\\delta^{N-1-a}\n \\quad(y_0\\in S(E),\\ \\delta>0).\\label{s:cap-hypothesis}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $$\\label{s:moment-margin}\n N-1-a-(h-1)-b\\ge1,$$ then $$\\label{s:moment-bound}\n \\mathbb E_L\\left(\\int f\\,d\\sigma_L\\right)^h\n \\le\\left([4\\max\\{A_0,9A_1\\}]^d\\Lambda\\right)^h.$$ For a nonempty family of at most $W$ functions satisfying the same conditions with numbers $\\Lambda_v$, $$\\label{s:moment-maximum}\n \\mathbb E_L\\max_v\\int f_v\\,d\\sigma_L\n \\le W^{1/h}[4\\max\\{A_0,9A_1\\}]^d\\max_v\\Lambda_v.$$ In particular, $A_0=1,A_1=4$ gives the constant $144^d$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Finite-state Gaussian stream** ([source](#stmt-s-model)): Fix integers $d\ge2$, $M\ge0$, and $T\ge0$, and an accuracy $0<\epsilon\le1/10$.
- **Uniform probability on a sphere** ([source](#s:affine-sphere)): For a Euclidean linear space $H$, write $S(H)$ for its unit sphere and $\sigma_H$ for uniform probability on that sphere.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-borel** [claim](#stmt-s-borel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-borel): Pick Borel versions of the kernels and couple original and Borel runs outside a null set. -> Borel kernels with the same joint law
- **step-coordinates** [claim](#stmt-s-coordinates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-coordinates): Gaussian representation and gamma fraction independence. -> beta law of projection length and independent directions
- **step-beta** [claim](#stmt-s-beta-identities) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-beta-identities): Beta integral and gamma fraction product. -> inverse moments and beta products
- **step-residual** [claim](#stmt-s-residual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-residual): Apply the coordinates lemma to the row span and its complement. -> residual subsphere representation
- **step-state-max** [claim](#stmt-s-state-maximum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-state-maximum): Bound the weighted sum by the maximum, then use the reversed-order residual law. -> expected maximum bound
- **step-caps** [claim](#stmt-s-caps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-caps): Integrate the coordinate density and recentre successful directions. -> cap mass bounds
- **step-directions** [claim](#stmt-s-directions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-directions): Gram-Schmidt with a uniform orthogonal matrix and a ratio of coordinate densities. -> conditional direction density
- **step-terminal-count** [claim](#stmt-s-terminal-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-terminal-count): Count terminal index-state pairs and bound each by a cap; pad with absorbing states. -> success and width bounds
- **step-short-run** [claim](#stmt-s-short-run) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-short-run): Take logarithms in the terminal count bound. -> dimension budget
- **step-full-data** [claim](#stmt-s-full-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-full-data): Markov inequality on the projection norm and a cap bound on the residual. -> full-data success bound
- **step-mixture** [claim](#stmt-s-mixture) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-mixture): Compare scalar beta densities on two ranges and lift to vector measures. -> two-component domination
- **step-main** [claim](#stmt-s-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-mixture-2): Contradiction with backward induction over blocks using the state maximum, mixture domination and beta moments. -> explicit sample lower bound
- **step-worst-signal** [claim](#stmt-s-worst-signal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-worst-signal): Integrate the guarantee over a uniform signal. -> bound for every-signal guarantees
- **step-cap-moment** [claim](#stmt-s-cap-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-cap-moment): Tube covering by caps, shell summation and reverse conditional integration. -> moment and maximum bounds
- **step-weighted** [claim](#stmt-s-weighted-theorem) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-cap-moment-2): Latitude tests supply cap hypotheses; apply the cap-moment lemma and a beta integral for the radius. -> radius-weighted block estimate
- **step-weighted-application** [claim](#stmt-s-weighted-application) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-weighted-application): Iterate the block estimate over padded blocks and take logarithms. -> precision rate at success one half
- **step-affine** [claim](#stmt-s-affine-proposition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-affine-proposition): Induction over blocks using latitude bounds, the cap-moment lemma and an integer beta product. -> all-affine suffix bound
- **step-fixed** [claim](#stmt-s-fixed-proposition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-s-fixed-proposition): Induction on the number of blocks with latitude simulation by a smaller Gaussian learner. -> dimension-parameterized success bound

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-sample-lower-bound**: For a memory sequence of subquadratic order, once the dimension is past a threshold that depends only on that sequence, any finite-state learner with constant uniform-prior angular success at a target accuracy needs a number of samples proportional to the dimension times the logarithm of the reciprocal accuracy, with an explicit constant coefficient.
- **inv-cap-success-bound**: On an affine subsphere of radius $R$ and linear dimension $D$, any fixed unit output reaches angular success at most the minimum of one and $(8\epsilon/R)^{D-1}$.
- **inv-terminal-count**: Success probability is bounded by the number of terminal index-state pairs times a single cap mass, so the persistent state set size enters the bound only through that count.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-unbounded-memory**: Applying the sample lower bound to streaming methods that can store a full set of d measurements or a full Gram matrix. -> With enough persistent memory the learner keeps d independent equations, which determine the signal almost surely, so no precision-dependent sample growth is forced.
- **anti-noisy-labels**: Treating the exact-label bound as a direct consequence of a lower bound proved for noisy labels, or the reverse. -> Exact labels carry more information than noisy ones, so a noisy-label bound does not transfer to the exact-label model.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are asymptotic lower bounds with thresholds in d that are only shown to exist, plus lemmas about probability laws on spheres; the source gives no finite procedure that computes an object, so there is nothing to execute and check.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-subquadratic-memory** (hypothesis): The sample lower bound is proved only when the persistent memory exponent grows slower than the square of the dimension; the dimension threshold depends on the memory sequence. Blocked use: ['Applying the bound to learners that store about d exact equations or a full Gram matrix', 'Quoting a memory-independent dimension threshold']
- **limit-exact-gaussian-model** (formal_scope): The result concerns exact noiseless labels from independent standard Gaussian rows and a finite persistent state; noisy labels or other row laws are not covered. Blocked use: ['Transferring the coefficient to noisy regression', 'Using it for structured or non-Gaussian measurement matrices']
- **limit-large-dimension-threshold** (edge_case): The conclusion holds only for dimensions above a threshold; accuracy up to one tenth is covered uniformly, but small dimensions are not controlled. Blocked use: ['Using the explicit coefficient at small d', 'Treating the threshold as an explicit number']
- **limit-success-probability** (hypothesis): The explicit coefficient is attached to uniform-prior angular success at least 2/3; the radius-weighted route at success one half gives only an unspecified absolute coefficient. Blocked use: ['Reading the explicit coefficient at other success levels', "Treating the weighted corollary's constant as computed"]
- **limit-short-run-regime** (nonconstructive): The dimension budget for the block induction is derived only on the hypothetical short-run sequence, and the argument is by contradiction; it produces no learner or matching upper bound. Blocked use: ['Reading the bound as tight', 'Extracting an algorithm from the proof']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 140 (`lean/docs/140.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Memory and precision in noiseless Gaussian regression](../../preprints/Memory-and-precision-in-noiseless-Gaussian-regression-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Posterior replicas and conditional information in Gaussian regression](../../preprints/Posterior-replicas-and-conditional-information-in-Gaussian-regression-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Localization costs and information growth for exact Gaussian observations](../../preprints/Localization-costs-and-information-growth-for-exact-Gaussian-observations-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Projection moments, positive cap domination, and Riesz estimates on the sphere](../../preprints/Projection-moments-positive-cap-domination-and-Riesz-estimates-on-the-sphere-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Replacing Gaussian observations in memory-constrained inference](../../preprints/Replacing-Gaussian-observations-in-memory-constrained-inference-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Subsphere methods for memory–sample lower bounds in noiseless Gaussian regression](../../preprints/Subsphere-methods-for-memory-sample-lower-bounds-in-noiseless-Gaussian-regression-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> In noiseless Gaussian regression, a learner observes inner products of an unknown unit vector in $\mathbb R^d$ with independent standard Gaussian vectors. The formalization proves that, for every fixed $A>0$, there is $c_A>0$ such that, in sufficiently large dimension, a learner retaining at most $Ad^2$ bits between observations needs at least $c_A d\log(1/\varepsilon)$ observations to attain angular error at most $\varepsilon$ with probability at least $2/3$ for a uniformly random unit signal, for $0<\varepsilon\le1/10$.
>
> For memory $o(d^2)$, the constant can be universal, and the selected statement also permits success at least $2/3$ separately for every signal. The linked supporting results include inverse Gram-matrix moments, exact-projection densities, regularization bounds, and sphere- and cube-prior block estimates.
>
> The paper studies how posterior replicas—independent signals drawn conditionally on the same observed data—control information in noiseless Gaussian regression. The formalization establishes the equal-label identity for finite measures, its density and measurable forms, and information bounds for Gaussian, distance, Haar, synthetic, and incidence comparisons. These statements retain exact observation labels, independent side randomness, and randomized transition rules.
>
> For a learner using $M(d)=o(d^2)$ bits of memory, the resulting bound says that, for sufficiently large $d$ and $0<\varepsilon\le1/10$, success probability at least $2/3$ under the uniform unit-sphere prior requires $T\ge c d\log(1/\varepsilon)$ observations, where $c>0$ is universal. The formalized streaming result uses the $2/3$ threshold; the stronger $3/5$ variant is outside this scope.
>
> The formalization controls information gained from exact Gaussian observations of a spherical image of the uniform cube. Under the stated entropy and size conditions, repeated localization over $t$ observation blocks produces nested disclosures with mutual information at most $C(n+1)t$ and expected final localization level at most $Ct$. The selected estimates include inverse-volume control, kernel coercivity, and drift for the actual observation rows.
>
> For a law of finite relative entropy with respect to a uniform cell law in sufficiently large dimension, the regularization procedure terminates almost surely in regular cells. Its expected depth and number of attempts are finite, with at most twice one plus the expected depth in expectation; the remaining relative entropy plus $(\log 2)n/4$ times the expected depth is at most the initial relative entropy plus $2/e$.
>
> The paper uses projection moments and domination by positive spherical caps to bound the information carried by exact Gaussian observations of an unknown unit vector. The formalization covers integrated frame and sphere moments, cap domination, Riesz-density estimates with fixed offsets, and bounds valid at every radius. It also covers probability bounds for finite-memory learners at a fixed stopping time and after summing over stopping times.
>
> For $M(d)=o(d^2)$ bits of memory, sufficiently large $d$, and $0<\varepsilon\le1/10$, success probability at least $2/3$ under the uniform sphere prior requires $T\ge c d\log(1/\varepsilon)$ observations for a universal $c>0$. A corresponding bound holds when $M\le A d^2$ for each fixed $A>0$, with the constant allowed to depend on $A$. The randomized learner model allows independent shared seeds; its reduction to a finite-state model is proved for dimensions at least three, with the auxiliary dimension-two case outside this scope.
>
> The paper compares fresh Gaussian observations with observations coupled to information already held in memory. The formalization covers mixed-moment and actual-row estimates, critical-radius, one-level, and two-label comparisons, and conditional-information bounds across a block of observations. For sufficiently large dimension $d$, the relevant entropy bounds of order $d^2$ bound the information in the fresh experiment by that in the coupled experiment plus a term of order $d$; the associated block information increase also has order $d$.
>
> It also covers two-point identities on observation fibers and the fiber-block bound, including the boundary case of $d-2$ observed rows. The uniform unit-sphere prior, Gaussian rows, finite memory states, and randomized transition rules remain part of these statements. The separate streaming consequences and several intermediate comparison constructions are outside this selection.
>
> The paper uses subspheres to bound the number of exact Gaussian observations needed to estimate a unit vector with limited memory. The formalization proves that, for $M(d)=o(d^2)$ bits of memory, sufficiently large $d$, and $0<\varepsilon\le1/10$, success probability at least $2/3$ under the uniform sphere prior, or for every unit signal, requires at least $2^{-16}d\log_2(1/\varepsilon)$ observations. Under the uniform prior, a lower bound of the same order with a universal positive constant holds at success probability $1/2$.
>
> The formalization also covers radius-weighted block and terminal estimates, affine and dimension estimates, and a vector-valued beta-mixture comparison with its boundary cases. These statements retain their dimension, width, radius, and suffix hypotheses, as well as the finite-state learner's independent randomness and restrictions on stopping and output.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

open MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory Set Filter
open scoped ENNReal NNReal RealInnerProductSpace
noncomputable section
universe u
namespace SubsphereCurrent
abbrev Vector (d : ℕ) := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin d)
abbrev UnitSphere (d : ℕ) := Metric.sphere (0 : Vector d) 1
abbrev State (M : ℕ) := Fin (2 ^ M)
abbrev Samples (d T : ℕ) := Fin T → Vector d

def uniformSphere (d : ℕ) : Measure (UnitSphere d) :=
  let σ := (volume : Measure (Vector d)).toSphere
  (σ Set.univ)⁻¹ • σ

def sampleLaw (d T : ℕ) : Measure (Samples d T) :=
  Measure.pi fun _ => stdGaussian (Vector d)

def observationReference (d : ℕ) : Measure (Vector d × ℝ) :=
  (stdGaussian (Vector d)).prod volume

structure KernelLearner (d M T : ℕ) where
  initial : PMF (Bool × State M)
  transition : ℕ → State M → (Vector d × ℝ) → PMF (Bool × State M)
  output : ℕ → State M → Measure (UnitSphere d)
  output_probability : ∀ t q, IsProbabilityMeasure (output t q)

namespace KernelLearner
abbrev RunStatus (M : ℕ) := Sum (State M) (ℕ × State M)

def tagged (M t : ℕ) (a : Bool × State M) : RunStatus M :=
  if a.1 then .inr (t, a.2) else .inl a.2

def run {d M T : ℕ} (L : KernelLearner d M T) (s : UnitSphere d)
    (x : Samples d T) : ℕ → PMF (RunStatus M)
  | 0 => L.initial.map (tagged M 0)
  | n + 1 => (L.run s x n).bind fun status =>
      match status with
      | .inr done => PMF.pure (.inr done)
      | .inl q => if hn : n < T then
          (L.transition (n + 1) q (x ⟨n, hn⟩, ⟪x ⟨n, hn⟩, s.val⟫)).map (tagged M (n + 1))
        else PMF.pure (.inl q)

def terminal {d M T : ℕ} (L : KernelLearner d M T) (s : UnitSphere d)
    (x : Samples d T) : PMF (ℕ × State M) :=
  (L.run s x T).map fun status =>
    match status with
    | .inl q => (T, q)
    | .inr done => done

def estimateLaw {d M T : ℕ} (L : KernelLearner d M T) (s : UnitSphere d)
    (x : Samples d T) : Measure (UnitSphere d) :=
  (L.terminal s x).toMeasure.bind fun a => L.output a.1 a.2

def angularError {d : ℕ} (s u : UnitSphere d) : ℝ :=
  Real.arccos ⟪u.val, s.val⟫

def conditionalSuccess {d M T : ℕ} (L : KernelLearner d M T) (ε : ℝ)
    (s : UnitSphere d) (x : Samples d T) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  L.estimateLaw s x {u | angularError s u ≤ ε}

def CompletedRules {d M T : ℕ} (L : KernelLearner d M T) : Prop :=
  ∀ t q dest, AEMeasurable (fun obs => L.transition t q obs dest) (observationReference d)

end KernelLearner

structure SeededLearner (d M T : ℕ) (Ω : Type u) where
  rules : Ω → KernelLearner d M T

namespace SeededLearner

structure Admissible {d M T : ℕ} {Ω : Type u} [MeasurableSpace Ω]
    (L : SeededLearner d M T Ω) (ρ : Measure Ω) : Prop where
  completed_rules : ∀ᵐ ω ∂ρ, (L.rules ω).CompletedRules
  fixed_experiment : ∀ s ε, AEMeasurable
    (fun z : Ω × Samples d T => (L.rules z.1).conditionalSuccess ε s z.2)
    (ρ.prod (sampleLaw d T))
  uniform_experiment : ∀ ε, AEMeasurable
    (fun z : UnitSphere d × (Ω × Samples d T) =>
      (L.rules z.2.1).conditionalSuccess ε z.1 z.2.2)
    ((uniformSphere d).prod (ρ.prod (sampleLaw d T)))

def success {d M T : ℕ} {Ω : Type u} [MeasurableSpace Ω]
    (L : SeededLearner d M T Ω) (ρ : Measure Ω) (ε : ℝ) (s : UnitSphere d) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ∫⁻ z : Ω × Samples d T, (L.rules z.1).conditionalSuccess ε s z.2
    ∂(ρ.prod (sampleLaw d T))

def uniformSuccess {d M T : ℕ} {Ω : Type u} [MeasurableSpace Ω]
    (L : SeededLearner d M T Ω) (ρ : Measure Ω) (ε : ℝ) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ∫⁻ z : UnitSphere d × (Ω × Samples d T),
    (L.rules z.2.1).conditionalSuccess ε z.1 z.2.2
      ∂((uniformSphere d).prod (ρ.prod (sampleLaw d T)))
end SeededLearner

def ExplicitPrecision : Prop :=
  ∀ M : ℕ → ℕ,
    Asymptotics.IsLittleO atTop (fun d => (M d : ℝ)) (fun d => (d : ℝ) ^ 2) →
    ∃ d₀ : ℕ, ∀ d : ℕ, d₀ ≤ d → ∀ T : ℕ, ∀ ε : ℝ,
    0 < ε → ε ≤ 1 / 10 →
    ∀ (Ω : Type u) [MeasurableSpace Ω] (ρ : Measure Ω), IsProbabilityMeasure ρ →
    ∀ L : SeededLearner d (M d) T Ω, L.Admissible ρ →
    ((2 / 3 : ℝ≥0∞) ≤ L.uniformSuccess ρ ε ∨
      ∀ s, (2 / 3 : ℝ≥0∞) ≤ L.success ρ ε s) →
    (2 : ℝ)⁻¹ ^ 16 * (d : ℝ) * Real.logb 2 (1 / ε) ≤ (T : ℝ)
end SubsphereCurrent
end

abbrev Vector (d : ℕ) := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin d)
abbrev Sphere (E : Type*) [NormedAddCommGroup E] := Metric.sphere (0 : E) 1

def sphereLaw (E : Type*) [NormedAddCommGroup E] [InnerProductSpace ℝ E]
    [FiniteDimensional ℝ E] [MeasurableSpace E] [BorelSpace E] : Measure (Sphere E) :=
  let σ := (volume : Measure E).toSphere
  (σ Set.univ)⁻¹ • σ

structure Test (d n : ℕ) where
  space : Submodule ℝ (Vector d)
  dimension : Module.finrank ℝ space = n
  center : Vector d
  perpendicular : center ∈ spaceᗮ
  radius : ℝ
  radius_positive : 0 < radius
  unit : ‖center‖^2 + radius^2 = 1

def Test.point {d n : ℕ} (A : Test d n) (s : Sphere A.space) : Vector d :=
  A.center + A.radius • s.val.val

end SubsphereCurrent.Affine

open MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory Set
open scoped ENNReal NNReal RealInnerProductSpace
noncomputable section
universe v
namespace SubsphereCurrent.Weighted
abbrev Vector (d : ℕ) := Affine.Vector d
abbrev Sphere (d : ℕ) := Metric.sphere (0 : Vector d) 1
abbrev Rows (d k : ℕ) := Fin k → Vector d
abbrev Observation (d k : ℕ) := Rows d k × (Fin k → ℝ)

def onTest {d n : ℕ} (A : Affine.Test d n) (s : Affine.Sphere A.space) : Sphere d := by
  refine ⟨A.point s,mem_sphere_zero_iff_norm.mpr ?_⟩
  have hs : ‖(s.val : Vector d)‖ = 1 := by
    rw [Submodule.norm_coe]
    exact mem_sphere_zero_iff_norm.mp s.property
  have ho : ⟪A.center, (s.val : Vector d)⟫ = 0 :=
    Submodule.inner_left_of_mem_orthogonal s.val.property A.perpendicular
  have hz : ‖A.point s‖^2 = 1 := by
    rw [Affine.Test.point,norm_add_sq_real,real_inner_smul_right,ho,mul_zero,norm_smul,
      Real.norm_eq_abs,abs_of_pos A.radius_positive,hs,mul_one]
    nlinarith [A.unit]
  nlinarith [norm_nonneg (A.point s)]

def norm (d n : ℕ) (g : Sphere d → ℝ≥0∞) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ⨆ A : Affine.Test d n, (ENNReal.ofReal A.radius)^((d : ℝ)/16) *
    ∫⁻ s : Affine.Sphere A.space, g (onTest A s) ∂Affine.sphereLaw A.space

def blockInput {d k : ℕ} {V : Type*} [Fintype V]
    (κ : V → Observation d k → ℝ≥0∞) (g : V → Sphere d → ℝ≥0∞)
    (s : Sphere d) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ∫⁻ X : Rows d k, ∑ v, κ v (X,fun i => ⟪X i,s.val⟫)*g v s
    ∂Measure.pi (fun _ => stdGaussian (Vector d))

def terminalInput {d : ℕ} (π : Measure (Sphere d)) (ε : ℝ) (s : Sphere d) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  π {u | Real.arccos ⟪u.val,s.val⟫ ≤ ε}

end SubsphereCurrent.Weighted
end

open MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory Set

namespace SubsphereCurrent.Weighted

def RadiusWeightedFullEstimate : Prop :=
  ∃ C : ℝ, 1 ≤ C ∧ ∃ d₀ : ℕ, ∀ d : ℕ, d₀ ≤ d →
    (∀ W : ℝ, 1 ≤ W → ∀ (V : Type v) [Fintype V] [Nonempty V],
      (Fintype.card V : ℝ) ≤ W →
      ∀ κ : V → Observation d (d/16) → ℝ≥0∞,
      (∀ v, Measurable (κ v)) → (∀ obs, ∑ v, κ v obs ≤ 1) →
      ∀ g : V → Sphere d → ℝ≥0∞,
      (∀ v, Measurable (g v)) → (∀ v s, g v s ≤ 1) →
      ∀ n : ℕ, (d : ℝ)/2 ≤ n → n ≤ d →
      norm d n (blockInput κ g) ≤
        (ENNReal.ofReal W)^(1/((d/16 : ℕ) : ℝ))*ENNReal.ofReal (C^d)*(⨆ v, norm d (n-2) (g v))) ∧
    (∀ n : ℕ, (d : ℝ)/2 ≤ n → n ≤ d →
      ∀ π : Measure (Sphere d), IsProbabilityMeasure π →
      ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε →
      norm d n (terminalInput π ε) ≤ ENNReal.ofReal (C^d)*(ENNReal.ofReal ε)^((d : ℝ)/16))

end SubsphereCurrent.Weighted

namespace SubsphereCurrent
namespace Fixed

abbrev Vector (m : ℕ) := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin m)
abbrev Sphere (E : Type*) [NormedAddCommGroup E] := Metric.sphere (0 : E) 1

def sphereLaw (E : Type*) [NormedAddCommGroup E] [InnerProductSpace ℝ E]
    [FiniteDimensional ℝ E] [MeasurableSpace E] [BorelSpace E] : Measure (Sphere E) :=
  let σ := (volume : Measure E).toSphere
  (σ Set.univ)⁻¹ • σ

def rowsLaw (E : Type*) [NormedAddCommGroup E] [InnerProductSpace ℝ E]
    [FiniteDimensional ℝ E] [MeasurableSpace E] [BorelSpace E] (T : ℕ) :
    Measure (Fin T → E) := Measure.pi fun _ => stdGaussian E

structure Stream (E J : Type*) [MeasurableSpace E] (T : ℕ) where
  initial : PMF J
  transition : ℕ → J → (E × ℝ) → PMF J
  output : J → Measure E
  output_probability : ∀ q, IsProbabilityMeasure (output q)

namespace Stream
variable {E J : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [InnerProductSpace ℝ E]
  [MeasurableSpace E] [MeasurableSpace J] [Fintype J] [MeasurableSingletonClass J]
  {T : ℕ}

def run (L : Stream E J T) (s : E) (x : Fin T → E) : ℕ → PMF J
  | 0 => L.initial
  | n+1 => (L.run s x n).bind fun q => if hn : n < T then
      L.transition (n+1) q (x ⟨n,hn⟩, ⟪x ⟨n,hn⟩,s⟫)
    else PMF.pure q

def estimateLaw (L : Stream E J T) (s : E) (x : Fin T → E) : Measure E :=
  (L.run s x T).toMeasure.bind L.output

def euclideanSuccess (L : Stream E J T) (η : ℝ) (s : E) (x : Fin T → E) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  L.estimateLaw s x {y | ‖y-s‖ ≤ η}

def angularSuccess (L : Stream E J T) (ε : ℝ) (s : E) (x : Fin T → E) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  L.estimateLaw s x {y | Real.arccos ⟪y,s⟫ ≤ ε}

def BorelRules (L : Stream E J T) : Prop :=
  ∀ n q a, Measurable (fun obs => L.transition n q obs a)

def suffix (L : Stream E J T) (n k : ℕ) (v : J) : Stream E J k where
  initial := PMF.pure v
  transition t := L.transition (n+t)
  output := L.output
  output_probability := L.output_probability

end Stream

def DimensionEstimate : Prop :=
  ∃ C : ℝ, 2 ≤ C ∧ ∃ d₀ : ℕ, ∀ d : ℕ, d₀ ≤ d → ∀ j m : ℕ,
    (d : ℝ)/2 + 2*j ≤ m → m ≤ d →
    ∀ η : ℝ, 0 < η →
    ∀ (Ω : Type u) [MeasurableSpace Ω] (ρ : Measure Ω), IsProbabilityMeasure ρ →
    ∀ L : Ω → Stream (Vector m) (Fin (2^(d^2))) (j*(d/32)),
    (∀ᵐ ω ∂ρ, (L ω).BorelRules) →
    AEMeasurable (fun z : Sphere (Vector m) × (Ω × (Fin (j*(d/32)) → Vector m)) =>
      (L z.2.1).euclideanSuccess η z.1.val z.2.2)
      ((sphereLaw (Vector m)).prod (ρ.prod (rowsLaw (Vector m) (j*(d/32))))) →
    (∫⁻ z : Sphere (Vector m) × (Ω × (Fin (j*(d/32)) → Vector m)),
      (L z.2.1).euclideanSuccess η z.1.val z.2.2
        ∂(sphereLaw (Vector m)).prod (ρ.prod (rowsLaw (Vector m) (j*(d/32)))))
      ≤ ENNReal.ofReal ((C^(j+1)*η)^((d : ℝ)/32))

end Fixed
end SubsphereCurrent
end

abbrev Orthogonal (E : Type*) [NormedAddCommGroup E] [InnerProductSpace ℝ E] [FiniteDimensional ℝ E] :=

instance orthogonal_compact : CompactSpace (Orthogonal E) := by
  apply isCompact_iff_compactSpace.mp
  apply (isCompact_closedBall (0 : E →L[ℝ] E) 1).of_isClosed_subset isClosed_unitary

namespace SubsphereCurrent.Mixture
variable {E : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [InnerProductSpace ℝ E] [FiniteDimensional ℝ E]

def blockIso (V : Submodule ℝ E) (u : Orthogonal V) (w : Orthogonal Vᗮ) : E ≃ₗᵢ[ℝ] E :=
  (V.orthogonalDecomposition.trans
    ((Unitary.linearIsometryEquiv u).withLpProdCongr 2 (Unitary.linearIsometryEquiv w))).trans
      V.orthogonalDecomposition.symm
end SubsphereCurrent.Mixture
end

open MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory Set
open scoped ENNReal NNReal RealInnerProductSpace
noncomputable section
namespace SubsphereCurrent.Mixture
variable {E : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [InnerProductSpace ℝ E] [FiniteDimensional ℝ E]
  [MeasurableSpace E] [BorelSpace E]

def directionLaw (V N : Submodule ℝ E) : Measure E :=
  ((orthogonalLaw (E := Vᗮ)).prod (SubsphereRegression.ambientSphereLaw (E := N))).map
    (fun z => blockIso V 1 z.1 (z.2 : E))

def directionJointLaw (V N : Submodule ℝ E) (t : ℕ) : Measure (Fin t → E) :=
  ((orthogonalLaw (E := Vᗮ)).prod
    (Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin t => SubsphereRegression.ambientSphereLaw (E := N)))).map
      (fun z i => blockIso V 1 z.1 (z.2 i : E))

def latitudeSpace (V : Submodule ℝ E) (e : Metric.sphere (0 : V) 1) : Submodule ℝ E :=
  (ℝ ∙ (e.val : E))ᗮ

def latitudeLaw (V : Submodule ℝ E) (e : Metric.sphere (0 : V) 1) (a delta : ℝ) : Measure E :=
  ((betaMeasure a delta).prod ((orthogonalLaw (E := V)).prod
    (SubsphereRegression.ambientSphereLaw (E := latitudeSpace V e)))).map
      (fun z => blockIso V z.2.1 1
        (Real.sqrt (1-z.1) • (e.val : E) + Real.sqrt z.1 • (z.2.2 : E)))

def MixtureEstimate (E : Type*) [NormedAddCommGroup E] [InnerProductSpace ℝ E]
    [FiniteDimensional ℝ E] [MeasurableSpace E] [BorelSpace E] : Prop :=
  (∀ (V N : Submodule ℝ E), V ≤ N → 1 ≤ Module.finrank ℝ V →
    Module.finrank ℝ V < Module.finrank ℝ N → Module.finrank ℝ N < Module.finrank ℝ E →
    ∀ e : Metric.sphere (0 : V) 1,
    directionLaw V N ≤ (2 : ℝ≥0∞) ^ Module.finrank ℝ E •
      (SubsphereRegression.ambientSphereLaw + latitudeLaw V e
        (((Module.finrank ℝ N : ℝ)-(Module.finrank ℝ V : ℝ))/2)
        (((Module.finrank ℝ E : ℝ)-(Module.finrank ℝ N : ℝ))/2))) ∧
  (∀ N : Submodule ℝ E, 1 ≤ Module.finrank ℝ N →
    directionLaw ⊥ N = SubsphereRegression.ambientSphereLaw) ∧
  (∀ (V : Submodule ℝ E) (t : ℕ), 1 ≤ Module.finrank ℝ E →
    directionJointLaw V ⊤ t =
      Measure.pi (fun _ : Fin t => SubsphereRegression.ambientSphereLaw))

end SubsphereCurrent.Mixture
end

noncomputable section
universe u v w
namespace SubsphereCurrent

def CurrentMainScope : Prop :=
  ExplicitPrecision.{u} ∧
  Weighted.RadiusWeightedFullEstimate.{v} ∧
  HalfPrecision.{u} ∧
  Affine.AllAffineFullEstimate.{u,v} ∧
  Fixed.DimensionEstimate.{u} ∧
  (∀ (E : Type w) [NormedAddCommGroup E] [InnerProductSpace ℝ E]
    [FiniteDimensional ℝ E] [MeasurableSpace E] [BorelSpace E], Mixture.MixtureEstimate E)

end SubsphereCurrent
end

noncomputable section
universe u v w
namespace SubsphereCurrent

theorem full_current_main_scope : CurrentMainScope.{u,v,w} := by
  sorry

end SubsphereCurrent
end

end OAI

    "text": "The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):\n\n- [Memory and precision in noiseless Gaussian regression](../../preprints/Memory-and-precision-in-noiseless-Gaussian-regression-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Posterior replicas and conditional information in Gaussian regression](../../preprints/Posterior-replicas-and-conditional-information-in-Gaussian-regression-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Localization costs and information growth for exact Gaussian observations](../../preprints/Localization-costs-and-information-growth-for-exact-Gaussian-observations-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Projection moments, positive cap domination, and Riesz estimates on the sphere](../../preprints/Projection-moments-positive-cap-domination-and-Riesz-estimates-on-the-sphere-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Replacing Gaussian observations in memory-constrained inference](../../preprints/Replacing-Gaussian-observations-in-memory-constrained-inference-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Subsphere methods for memory–sample lower bounds in noiseless Gaussian regression](../../preprints/Subsphere-methods-for-memory-sample-lower-bounds-in-noiseless-Gaussian-regression-September-27-2026/paper.pdf)\n\n## Scope\n\nIn noiseless Gaussian regression, a learner observes inner products of an unknown unit vector in $\\mathbb R^d$ with independent standard Gaussian vectors. The formalization proves that, for every fixed $A>0$, there is $c_A>0$ such that, in sufficiently large dimension, a learner retaining at most $Ad^2$ bits between observations needs at least $c_A d\\log(1/\\varepsilon)$ observations to attain angular error at most $\\varepsilon$ with probability at least $2/3$ for a uniformly random unit signal, for $0<\\varepsilon\\le1/10$.\n\nFor memory $o(d^2)$, the constant can be universal, and the selected statement also permits success at least $2/3$ separately for every signal. The linked supporting results include inverse Gram-matrix moments, exact-projection densities, regularization bounds, and sphere- and cube-prior block estimates.\n\nThe paper studies how posterior replicas—independent signals drawn conditionally on the same observed data—control information in noiseless Gaussian regression. The formalization establishes the equal-label identity for finite measures, its density and measurable forms, and information bounds for Gaussian, distance, Haar, synthetic, and incidence comparisons. These statements retain exact observation labels, independent side randomness, and randomized transition rules.\n\nFor a learner using $M(d)=o(d^2)$ bits of memory, the resulting bound says that, for sufficiently large $d$ and $0<\\varepsilon\\le1/10$, success probability at least $2/3$ under the uniform unit-sphere prior requires $T\\ge c d\\log(1/\\varepsilon)$ observations, where $c>0$ is universal. The formalized streaming result uses the $2/3$ threshold; the stronger $3/5$ variant is outside this scope.\n\nThe formalization controls information gained from exact Gaussian observations of a spherical image of the uniform cube. Under the stated entropy and size conditions, repeated localization over $t$ observation blocks produces nested disclosures with mutual information at most $C(n+1)t$ and expected final localization level at most $Ct$. The selected estimates include inverse-volume control, kernel coercivity, and drift for the actual observation rows.\n\nFor a law of finite relative entropy with respect to a uniform cell law in sufficiently large dimension, the regularization procedure terminates almost surely in regular cells. Its expected depth and number of attempts are finite, with at most twice one plus the expected depth in expectation; the remaining relative entropy plus $(\\log 2)n/4$ times the expected depth is at most the initial relative entropy plus $2/e$.\n\nThe paper uses projection moments and domination by positive spherical caps to bound the information carried by exact Gaussian observations of an unknown unit vector. The formalization covers integrated frame and sphere moments, cap domination, Riesz-density estimates with fixed offsets, and bounds valid at every radius. It also covers probability bounds for finite-memory learners at a fixed stopping time and after summing over stopping times.\n\nFor $M(d)=o(d^2)$ bits of memory, sufficiently large $d$, and $0<\\varepsilon\\le1/10$, success probability at least $2/3$ under the uniform sphere prior requires $T\\ge c d\\log(1/\\varepsilon)$ observations for a universal $c>0$. A corresponding bound holds when $M\\le A d^2$ for each fixed $A>0$, with the constant allowed to depend on $A$. The randomized learner model allows independent shared seeds; its reduction to a finite-state model is proved for dimensions at least three, with the auxiliary dimension-two case outside this scope.\n\nThe paper compares fresh Gaussian observations with observations coupled to information already held in memory. The formalization covers mixed-moment and actual-row estimates, critical-radius, one-level, and two-label comparisons, and conditional-information bounds across a block of observations. For sufficiently large dimension $d$, the relevant entropy bounds of order $d^2$ bound the information in the fresh experiment by that in the coupled experiment plus a term of order $d$; the associated block information increase also has order $d$.\n\nIt also covers two-point identities on observation fibers and the fiber-block bound, including the boundary case of $d-2$ observed rows. The uniform unit-sphere prior, Gaussian rows, finite memory states, and randomized transition rules remain part of these statements. The separate streaming consequences and several intermediate comparison constructions are outside this selection.\n\nThe paper uses subspheres to bound the number of exact Gaussian observations needed to estimate a unit vector with limited memory. The formalization proves that, for $M(d)=o(d^2)$ bits of memory, sufficiently large $d$, and $0<\\varepsilon\\le1/10$, success probability at least $2/3$ under the uniform sphere prior, or for every unit signal, requires at least $2^{-16}d\\log_2(1/\\varepsilon)$ observations. Under the uniform prior, a lower bound of the same order with a universal positive constant holds at success probability $1/2$.\n\nThe formalization also covers radius-weighted block and terminal estimates, affine and dimension estimates, and a vector-valued beta-mixture comparison with its boundary cases. These statements retain their dimension, width, radius, and suffix hypotheses, as well as the finite-state learner's independent randomness and restrictions on stopping and output."

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Let a finite-state streaming learner estimate a uniformly random unit vector from independent exact Gaussian linear measurements. We prove that $`o(d^2)`$ bits of persistent memory and angular success probability at least 2/3 require at least $`2^{-16}d\log_2(1/\epsilon)`$ samples for all sufficiently large <i>d</i>, uniformly for $`0\lt \epsilon\le1/10`$. The proof conditions each batch on its observed projection and controls the resulting random residual subsphere.
